And the right foot of a Tortoise <note>Latin: *Testudo*</note> tied upon the right foot of one suffering from gout <note>Latin: *podagricus*; a painful form of inflammatory arthritis</note> settles the pain; likewise, the left foot will help the left, and the hand can similarly benefit the hand. But the wise men maintain that these effects of stones are even more efficacious if you possess Solar <note>Latin: *Phæbeos*; referring to Phoebus Apollo</note> or Lunar stones; if you hang the former by a silver thread and the latter by a gold one around the neck, they say they are always nourished by the rays of the Sun or Moon. For the Moon-stone <note>Latin: *Selenitis*</note> not only imitates the shape of the Moon, but its motion, because it circles with it; worn around the neck, it produces a lunar spirit and flows with the virtues of the Moon. So too the stone named Sun-parsley-stone <note>Latin: *Helioselino*; likely referring to a variety of Jasper or a specific crystalline structure associated with both luminaries</note>, which displays the conjunction of both lights; when worn, it makes one a master of the virtues of both. Observe this principle in all other things. These are the matters we have gathered from the books of the ancients, which we have often used when in need, as is seen throughout this entire work.

### On the powers of gems, and their engraved images.
### CHAPTER XXI.

Since it is a different matter to transcribe and transfer elsewhere the topic of images engraved on gems and their virtues—as they do not operate by purely natural means—I have nevertheless placed them here to satisfy those greedy for a richer teaching of occult operations <note>Latin: *occultarum operationum*; hidden workings of nature</note>. I did this because I observed that the effects they demonstrate arise from natural virtue, aided and corroborated by the hours, configurations, and celestial characters, when they are inscribed with signs conforming to the virtues of the stones.

-># FOURTH BOOK<-

Many things have already been left to posterity by the ancients, yet much was also overlooked; nor does the more recent age labor any less in interpreting monsters <note>The author likely refers to "monstra" as omens or unnatural portents</note> and understanding their powers. But before I teach what must be said, I recall from memory many who have recorded these things. Ptolemy <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century astronomer and geographer</note> asserts that the appearances of this world are subject to the celestial faces, by which wise men performed wonders while constructing and describing images. And Hali Aben Rhodam <note>Ali ibn Ridwan, an Egyptian Muslim physician and astrologer</note>, a certain Egyptian sage, is said to have healed a man suffering from a scorpion's bite by using a seal of a scorpion impressed upon incense; he wore the image of a scorpion on a ring, and had ordered it to be engraved while the constellation was occupying the midheaven or the rising point of the horizon <note>The "ascendant"</note> and joined with the Moon. A similar story is told by Serapion. Porphyry <note>A Neoplatonic philosopher</note> also affirms that an image useful against serpents can be made when the Moon enters the celestial Serpent, or when it looks upon it favorably—and many other things which we omit for the sake of brevity. Here we shall briefly review the required operations and configurations.

To begin the matter, then, we find these things were written by the ancients for multiple reasons, as far as our knowledge has been able to extend. First, they were enclosed in rings so that letters might be closed with such a seal, and by this, the face of the sender might be recognized. Suetonius Tranquillus <note>Roman historian and author of 'The Twelve Caesars'</note> reported this regarding Augustus, who used a sphinx for signing diplomas, petitions, and letters; later he used the image of Alexander the Great, and finally his own image, carved by the hand of Dioscorides, which the following emperors also continued to use for signing. Ovid also reported the same in his *Tristia*. And often, those which are found among us and fall into our hands are seen inscribed with various human faces; these were most commonly made from sardonyx <note>A variety of onyx used in cameos and seals</note>, because it is the only stone that does not pull away the wax <note>original: "conuellat"</note>.

<summary>This page discusses the historical use of astrological seals and engraved rings for both healing and official identification, citing authorities like Ptolemy, Hali, and Suetonius. It highlights the transition from using mythical symbols like the Sphinx to personal portraits for royal seals.</summary>

*...they may be plucked away. There are also found writings against various accidents, strengthened by appropriate figurations of the stones' power, so that the operation of the stone may exist as more powerful and vivid. For among other things which are able to receive celestial influences, gems are particularly suitable; for although they may seem too hard for receiving celestial gifts, yet when they do receive them, they retain them more tenaciously and for a longer time—an opinion I see was also pleasing to Iamblichus <note>A Neoplatonist philosopher (c. 245–325 AD) who wrote extensively on theurgy and the divine power within material objects.</note>. Thus, we often find gems set in rings with which they are marked and decorated, for on the inside, where the stone touches the bare flesh of the finger, there are seen inscribed images, characters, and marks of various letters and words, written at opportune hours and times. You will understand the method of figuring and writing these more extensively in the following sections. Now, however, we shall briefly set down the virtues of stones suitable for our work, so that the matter may become clearer than the midday light through examples.*

### On the virtues of stones.
### CHAPTER XXII.

Now we shall review the virtues of stones; however, do not think that I am reporting all the virtues of all stones, for that would be a matter of labor rather than of genius and subtlety. Instead, I report only those which are more frequently found, which we have proven by experience, and whose figures correspond to the property of the stone and seem necessary to our work. You will easily find many full volumes on these matters that treat nothing else. And first, **Agate** <note>original: "Achates"</note>, which is found on the banks of the river Achates <note>The Dirillo river in Sicily, where these stones were famously found in antiquity.</note>, is black, smeared with white zones, and sometimes washed with glittering drops. It is effective against the bites of scorpions and serpents; it makes a man eloquent and pleasing, and wins the friendship of kings.

# BOOK FOUR

<note>original: "LIBER QVARTVS"</note>

It reconciles. Therefore, we read that *Ismenias the Flute-player* <note>Ismenias was a famous 4th-century BC musician from Thebes</note> was accustomed to using many bright stones; and in *Persia*, by the smoke of these stones, storms are turned away and rivers are halted. The proof of this is said to be that if they are thrown into boiling cauldrons, they cool them down—though here such powers are considered vain. The *Alectorius* <note>The "cock-stone," a small crystal supposedly found in the gizzards of old roosters</note> is extracted from the belly of a rooster that has lived as a castrate for four years; however, we have extracted them from the stomach of an old hen. Held in the mouth, it extinguishes thirst. The wearer of this stone will be unconquered; it also helps one acquire honors, renders one eloquent, and makes a wife pleasing to her husband. In the same way, the *geranites* is extracted from the crane, the *draconites* or *drachetias* from the dragon, and the *borax* <note>Also known as the "toadstone," believed to be found in the heads of old toads</note> from the toad, which frees one from poison. Likewise, the *chelidonius* <note>Swallow-stone</note> is taken from the dissected bellies of swallows; but unless it is cut out while they are still living, it never becomes a gemstone, for if the serpent or animal dies first, the stone's power vanishes at the same time. Thus, if the stones are extracted while the creatures are still alive, those same stones retain the effects of the stars to which they are subject. For the *Alectorius* has solar power, which is why it makes those carrying it unconquered. Similarly, the *chelidonius* taken from swallows cures melancholy and makes one lovable, since it is of the nature of Jupiter; and you shall observe the same in others. The *Aëtites* <note>The "eagle-stone," a hollow geode with a smaller stone loose inside</note> when shaken, sounds with a little stone inside its belly; it assists pregnant women so they do not miscarry and soothes the suffering of those with the falling sickness <note>epilepsy</note>. The *Amethyst* is of a purple and violet brilliance. Named as if "not drunk" <note>from the Greek 'a-methystos'</note>, it resists drunkenness during drinking bouts, makes a prudent man out of a drunkard, and helps one devoted to letters. Because it can be easily carved, many figures are found engraved upon it, as will be discussed; it makes a man vigilant and provides good understanding to the wearer. *Coral* is beneficial in many things; therefore, people especially use the wearing of corals for warding off dangers and enchantments.

When worn, it grants a good reputation, stops flowing blood, drives away poisons, and does not allow the wearer to be deceived, keeping them safe. **Hyacinth** <note>likely a variety of zircon or sapphire</note> puts poisons and pestilential regions to flight; the Carthaginians <note>original: "Punici"</note> hand down the tradition that he who wears the hyacinth is safe from lightning. **Jasper** renders one chaste, stops blood and the menstrual flow, and is beneficial to those with dropsy <note>original: "aqua intercus"; an accumulation of fluid under the skin</note> and those whom fever burns; it makes one victorious and powerful against enemies and strengthens the stomach if hung raw from the neck so that it touches the mouth of the stomach—specifically the green variety. There are other inferior types that enjoy the name only by reputation. **Iris** <note>likely quartz crystal or rainbow spar</note> ends in a six-sided point, slightly tapered on both sides; if it is placed under a roof facing the rays of the sun while part of it remains in shadow, it displays the likeness of a heavenly rainbow upon the walls. This happens because of its shape, lest some think it happens of its own accord; for this reason, it aids a woman in labor. **Lapis Cyanus** <note>Lapis Lazuli</note> is effective against melancholy, quartan fever, and fainting. **Sapphire**, which appears to contain golden dust and shines with golden specks, preserves the limbs in a vigorous state, allows one to overcome the envious, and the wearer shall not be affected by fear; it cools fevers and heals inflammations, carbuncles, and cold abscesses by touch. The *Emerald* <note>original: "Smaragdus"</note>, distinguished by its green color, refreshes the sight of the eyes with its verdancy; for this reason, it is beneficial to gaze upon it, especially for those whose vision has been dulled by the glare of other gems. It is rarely found engraved, nor was it lightly decided to leave it so, lest its striking beauty be marred by the hollows of images, as it is difficult to carve. It must be worn chastely, for it does not endure sexual intercourse; as *Albertus* <note>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280), a medieval scholar and bishop</note> writes, the emerald of the King of Hungary shattered into pieces and broke during the hour of his congress with his wife. If it is concave, like others of its kind, it shows a reflected image turned upside down.

->Concerning the images of the Heavens and the Planets.<-

### CHAPTER XXIII.

THESE are the figures of the planets and the images of the heavens, which you will often find inscribed in conformity with the virtues of the stones: such as *Mercury*, a slender youth carrying a caduceus <note>a herald's staff entwined with two serpents</note>, with winged sandals on his head and feet. *Mars*, however, is a soldier in breastplate, warlike, carrying a banner, a spear, or a shield with him. *Venus* is a naked woman, holding a mirror and having with her the boy Cupid in a playful gesture. Likewise, *Jupiter* is sitting on a throne, commanding with a rod; *Saturn* is a scythe-bearing old man; and the *Sun* is distinguished by rays. No less frequently may one perceive the configurations of the eighth sphere <note>the sphere of the fixed stars, beyond the planets</note> upon these stones: the ape, the bear, the crown, the swan, the eagle, the winged horse, the serpent-bearer, and the rest. So too all the signs: rams, bulls, twins, crabs, lions, and the others; and from the natures of the stars to the planets

There are also other configurations of images with which the ancients used to fashion stones, according to the opinions of the Indians, Egyptians, Magi, and other astrologers. These are not so much visible as they are imaginable. For example, they say that in the first face <note>A "face" or decan is a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign.</note> of Aries, a black man of immense body ascends, having red eyes and girded with a white cloth. In the second face, a woman ascends covered in a linen cloak, girded with green cloths, holding up one foot. In the third, however, a man ascends dressed in red garments, having a golden bracelet on his hands, desiring to do good but unable to do so. Similar descriptions are assigned to the rest of the signs; whoever seeks these should consult their specific books, for it would be a long and overly tedious reading to review their opinions, which differ among themselves. Therefore, stones are found engraved with diverse and various images. There are others who work differently, for they express the matter through human figures and gestures. For instance, if they desire to induce love, they depict sweet embraces, gestures, and speeches, and people kissing. But if they desire hatred or anger, they depict them turning their backs, fleeing from one another, and shrinking away in horror. Thus they express all the passions of the soul which they hope to induce through figures. However, they engrave these in configurations that conform to the virtue of the stones and are appropriate to them; how these ought to be adapted and found is revealed in the following sections.

On Hyacinth <note>A zircon or sapphire variant</note>, lightning bolts are carved, so that it may keep those wearing it safe from its fire. ### The Elections Necessary for Inscribing Stones.
### CHAPTER XXV.

BUT they say that stones receive and seize a greater power from heaven if they are inscribed at fixed and opportune times; for in this way they are animated, and the starry configurations flow into them more strongly. They establish this as the foundation and root of everything: for if you desire to stir up love, use appropriate and benevolent dispositions; if you wish to kindle hatred, use hostile and destructive ones. Once the desired effect is known, the time is prepared. For if they decide to make images of Venus or Saturn, they wait for the former to enter Taurus or Libra, and the latter to enter Aquarius or Capricorn. <note>These are the "Dignities" or home signs of these planets in traditional astrology.</note> And so that the truth may be revealed, we find in their carvings the Sun in Leo, the Moon in Cancer, and Mercury in Gemini and Virgo. Thus, if they wish to inscribe a figure of Leo or Cancer, they observe the Sun and Moon wandering into them and inscribe them immediately. However, they especially take care that the Moon is "immune" and free from every impediment—namely from Mars and Saturn, as well as from the "combustion" of the Sun. <note>A planet is "combust" when it is too close to the Sun, which was thought to weaken its power.</note> Furthermore, they apply their minds to ensure the Moon is not "void of course," but rather wax—

# BOOK FOUR

<note>original: "LIBER QVARTVS"</note>

...swift and quick, and not found in a significant "heel" <note>likely referring to the end of a zodiacal sign or a specific degree</note> (for these are often the boundaries of misfortunes). If it is in a fortunate house, let the lucky planets behold it in a triangle or hexagon <note>trine or sextile aspects in astrology</note>, with the ascendant at the hinge of the rising <note>the Eastern Horizon</note> or at the summit of heaven <note>the Midheaven</note>, not in the falling houses. It should not be declining, for then a planet grieves and grows sluggish. Let the rising signs be diurnal by day, and nocturnal by night, so that one may obtain the matter more easily and no impediment can be brought against it. Conversely, you should proceed in the opposite manner if you are striving to induce hatred or infirmity. When they seek to engrave the figure of the signs, you will find no less through the following triplicities <note>groups of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element</note>: for the first triplicity consists of Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius, of which the Sun is lord by day, Jupiter by night, and in the twilight the cold-making star of Saturn. With such signs and carvings, they used to heal cold ailments, such as dropsy <note>edema or swelling</note> and paralysis; thus, according to the remaining triplicities, they engrave the other signs to heal other infirmities. Yet I shall not omit this: the multitude of all ages testifies that their power is fading and being extinguished. Therefore, those things which were frequently done by our ancestors are now seen to be utterly useless and vain.

Now, **King Philip**, best of men, we have finished our task on *Natural Magic* as far as our weakness allowed, and from this point on we shall rest from this genre, in which it seemed right to write things not so much wonderful as true. If anything remains unattempted, or is said awkwardly, let it be ascribed both to the difficulty of the business and the shortness of time. For these things are but the preludes to what must be said; we shall write of more sublime and secret things, if the divine powers favor us.

->**THE END.**<-

# WE HAVE ADDED THESE ENTRIES FROM PICTORIUS, SINCE THE PAGE WOULD OTHERWISE HAVE REMAINED BLANK.
<note>Georgius Pictorius (c. 1500–1569) was a physician and author known for his writings on medicine and "natural magic."</note>

Know that a drunkard recovers immediately and is healed—even if his head aches so much that his senses falter—if he places his testicles in cold water or washes them with diluted vinegar.

Foul-smelling wine is improved if you hang a proper proportion of clean wheat (according to the size of the vessel) into the wine using a small bag; for the wheat attracts whatever stench is in the wine and clarifies it wonderfully.

Distilled wine <note>original: "Vinum sublimatum," likely referring to brandy or high-proof alcohol</note> in which a small amount of salt has been mixed, if set alight in a dish after other lights have been extinguished, makes the people present look wonderfully terrified <note>The blue flame of burning alcohol creates a ghostly, pale appearance on human skin</note>; even a live crab turns red in this wine, even if no salt is added.

If a little rennet is added to the brain of a weasel, it ensures that cheese does not rot, even if it is three months old; nor will mice gnaw on such cheese.

If two pounds of saltpeter <term>sal nitri: potassium nitrate, often used in early chemistry and for cooling</term> are immersed in a bucket of pipe-water, the water will become cold instantly.
